# Underwrap codebase review program handover

## Purpose

This file is the durable control record for remediating the accepted whole-codebase
review. It allows the controller to resume safely across sessions without
reconstructing approvals, ordering, ticket outcomes, cleanup effects,
cross-ticket constraints, or validation history from chat context.

## Source report and baseline

- Source report: `tickets/_review/codebase_2026-07-18.md`
- Report date: 2026-07-18
- Reviewed baseline commit: `4c242f8e31428186b71f1da407cbfa7c58cf4198`
- Baseline manifest SHA-256: `803bedb30fe30812e98a305cf37798a50a683d7adf3e6961328ac741eed3fbba`
- Accepted finding scope: all final findings F-001–F-058.
- Report totals: 4 critical, 7 high, 26 medium, and 21 low findings.
- The report is engineering evidence, not formal verification, a penetration
  test, protocol certification, or an independent security audit.

Before materializing a ticket, compare the current tree with this baseline and
revalidate findings affected by intervening changes. The source report remains
the authority for full evidence, locations, consequences, recommendations,
themes, and validation notes.

## Scope and non-goals

### Scope

- Eventually assess and resolve every accepted final finding F-001–F-058 through
  approved, coherent, sequential tickets.
- Preserve finding priority, security-boundary meaning, dependencies, and the
  report's distinction between corrections and intentional behavior choices.
- Before each approved ticket's fix, deliberately seek valuable behavior-
  preserving simplifications across the repository and commit accepted cleanup
  as independently reviewed units.
- Maintain enough durable state here for every later ticket and decision to use
  outcomes from previous cleanup and fix work.

### Non-goals

- This bootstrap does not derive ticket filenames, hierarchy, grouping, or
  implementation tasks. It identifies F-001 only as the next candidate.
- There is no one-time broad refactor before review remediation. Optional
  formatting or renaming without concrete simplification value is not cleanup.
- Opportunities O-001–O-026 are not findings and are not approved behavior
  changes. They require separate explicit behavior approval.
- Unresolved items U-001–U-011 are not findings or tickets. They require the
  evidence or decision identified by the report and separate approval before
  work is created from them.
- Report acceptance does not approve dependencies, changes to the
  `underwrap-exec → underwrap-ns` binary chain, weakened security boundaries, or
  any other decision that `AGENTS.md` requires the user to approve.
- Do not include secret values, credentials, resource blobs, tokens, or key
  material in this file, tickets, commits, logs, or prompts.

## Immutable operating protocol

1. The parent/controller is the sole agent/controller decision-maker, subject to
   the user approvals and conditions in this protocol. It owns delegation,
   synthesis, scope control, and final repository state; assigned writer and
   fix-worker subagents edit only within their approved role/scope.
2. Begin directly in critical. Process severity in order critical → high →
   medium → low. There is no critical-entry gate. Immediately before each
   transition critical → high, high → medium, and medium → low, use `ask_user`
   with comment entry enabled and record the response. Do not transition without
   approval.
3. A lower-severity finding may move earlier only when it is a necessary
   prerequisite for current approved work. Record the dependency, necessity,
   controller decision, and resulting order in the decision log and ledger.
4. Materialize exactly one ticket file at a time—normally one task-only ticket,
   with no simultaneous parent/child hierarchy—under the existing ticket
   workflow. During materialization, this named program may write only that one
   todo ticket and this handover; the report, source, tests, configuration, and
   published docs remain read-only. Then stop and use `ask_user` with comment
   entry enabled for explicit approval before implementation or an in-progress
   move.
5. Ticket approval is explicit and ticket-specific. Report acceptance, a band-
   transition approval, generic continuation, or ticket creation is not
   implementation approval. Record approval comments and conditions faithfully.
   Rejection or requested changes leave the ticket out of in-progress and permit
   only revision/re-presentation of that same file, or stopping—not creation of
   another ticket.
6. After approval, run a cleanup-discovery phase before the actual finding fix.
   Direct the implementation worker to seek ticket-local and repository-wide
   behavior-preserving simplifications with concrete net reduction in
   complexity, duplication, or dead structure. Keep this phase attached to the
   current fix-led ticket; do not launch a broad preliminary refactor.
7. Reject optional formatting/renaming and cleanup without concrete reduction.
   Every cleanup must be coherent, independently reversible, and always
   behavior-preserving. User approval does not convert behavior-changing or
   boundary-weakening work into cleanup; handle it only as separately approved
   fix or Opportunity scope. Cleanup must not add dependencies, change the
   `underwrap-exec → underwrap-ns` chain, expose secrets, or settle/absorb an
   unresolved decision without `ask_user`. Opportunities and U-items retain
   separate approval.
8. For each accepted simplification, isolate one coherent cleanup unit. Give it
   a fresh independent review, a separate fix-worker pass if the controller
   accepts review corrections, focused and boundary-required validation, and a
   controller acceptance/sanity/scope/diff/security check. Then commit it alone
   as type `cleanup`, with the corresponding handover update, before any finding
   fix commit. If corrections require another coherent commit, classify it as
   cleanup and repeat the full cycle.
9. The first committed unit after ticket approval—first cleanup commit when any
   is accepted, otherwise the first fix commit—must move the ticket from todo to
   in-progress and make that state and approval durable. An approval-pending todo
   ticket may remain untracked: preserve its content at the in-progress path and
   include the old todo path in the staged allowlist only if it was tracked.
   Otherwise stage the new in-progress path, this handover, and the coherent
   implementation paths.
10. After cleanup is exhausted or explicitly records that no valuable cleanup
    was accepted, implement the finding correction in one or more coherent fix
    units. Each receives fresh independent review, a separate fix-worker pass
    for controller-accepted corrections, focused and boundary-required
    validation, and the controller's independent checks before its `fix` commit.
11. A ticket may have multiple cleanup and fix commits. Record every
    implementation commit in order with type, scope, review, fixes, validation,
    controller check, affected findings, and hash/subject. The final fix commit
    moves the ticket to done and records final outcome/validation. Do not include
    unrelated paths in any commit.
12. After that final fix commit, present the completed outcome, ordered commits,
    validation, reviewer result, controller sanity check, handover decisions,
    and effects on later findings; then use `ask_user` with comments enabled. If
    changes are requested, record `completion changes requested`, move the same
    ticket back to in-progress, and run another coherent fix/review/validation/
    commit cycle. Do not prepare another ticket. If accepted, record acceptance
    and continuation authorization. Persist completion-review and transition
    responses in exact-allowlist handover-only `control` commits outside the
    ticket's ordered cleanup/fix sequence; these commits also replace preceding
    `SELF` placeholders with immutable hashes. A following severity-transition
    gate occurs only after completion acceptance.
13. If cleanup changes, invalidates, or resolves evidence for another F-id,
    immediately update that finding's ledger entry and record the cleanup commit
    and effect. On reaching that F-id, revalidate current evidence and link the
    completed work instead of creating a duplicate ticket. Do not treat this as
    approval for any Opportunity or U-item.
14. Use one implementation writer subagent, separate fresh reviewer subagent(s),
    and a separate fix-worker subagent for controller-accepted corrections.
    Repeat review/fix as needed. Stop for `ask_user` if an unapproved product,
    architecture, dependency, binary-chain, behavior, unresolved-item, or
    security-boundary decision is required, or a blocker prevents safe work.
15. The controller independently checks acceptance criteria, scope, sanity, the
    actual diff, security invariants, validation, and absence of unrelated work
    for every cleanup and fix unit. Subagent review does not replace this check.
16. Update this handover after ticket creation and approval, every cleanup/fix
    unit and review/fix round, every implementation commit, completion review,
    transition gate, and control commit. Later decisions must use the recorded
    outcomes and accumulated lessons.
17. The initial workflow-setup commit contains only `AGENTS.md`,
    `tickets/_review/codebase_2026-07-18.md`, and this handover file. No ticket,
    cleanup, or finding-fix work belongs in that commit.
18. Follow `.pi/skills/underwrap-ticket-workflow/SKILL.md` for ticket content,
    validation, moves, and exact staging. For this named program, all-58 scope
    supersedes normal low deferral; materialization's read-only rule is overridden
    only for the single new todo ticket plus this handover; and the cleanup-first
    multi-commit/control-commit protocol supersedes the one-commit task loop.
    All other safety and preflight rules remain binding.
19. The controller is expressly allowed—and, when a material concern exists,
    should—tell the user plainly when a proposal, request, ticket choice,
    implementation direction, or process step looks like a bad idea. State the
    concrete downside and a safer or simpler alternative early, preferably
    before the relevant approval gate. This candor
    does not itself waive an approval gate, convert behavior-changing work into
    cleanup, or permit a security-boundary/dependency/binary-chain exception.

## Session-resume checklist

1. Read `AGENTS.md`, this file, the complete source report, and
   `.pi/skills/underwrap-ticket-workflow/SKILL.md`.
2. Run `git status --short` and `git diff --cached --name-only`. Preserve
   pre-existing changes. Stop if unexplained staged or overlapping changes make
   exact-scope action unsafe; never unstage another user's work.
3. Compare current HEAD/relevant files with the baseline. Revalidate any selected
   or cleanup-affected finding touched by intervening commits.
4. Read current band, transition table, decision log, registry and ordered
   commit sequences, finding ledger, constraints, and lessons. Identify the last
   completed unit and apply its outcomes to the next decision.
5. Confirm there is at most one materialized incomplete program ticket. Search
   todo, in-progress, and done for overlap before creating anything.
6. If a ticket exists, verify its exact approval/comments, durable board state,
   cleanup state, completion-review state, and first incomplete registry step.
   A rejected or revision-requested todo ticket may only be revised and
   re-presented. A `completion changes requested` ticket returns from done to
   in-progress for the same ticket's next fix cycle. Never infer approval from
   status, chat, transition approval, or a done/committed board state.
7. If the latest ticket is committed but completion review is pending, present
   its outcome, ordered cleanup/fix commits, validation, reviewer result,
   controller check, handover decisions, and later-finding effects; then use
   `ask_user` with comments enabled. Persist the response in a handover-only
   control commit and resolve preceding `SELF` placeholders. Until explicit
   acceptance and continuation authorization are recorded, do not prepare or
   derive another ticket. If continuation crosses a band, perform and persist
   the distinct transition gate afterward.
8. If an approved ticket is still in todo, the next committed cleanup or fix
   unit must include its move to in-progress. If cleanup is incomplete, finish
   cleanup discovery and each accepted cleanup's independent cycle before any
   actual finding fix.
9. If no ticket exists, ensure the current band is active (or its transition is
   approved), choose the smallest coherent next work after fresh evidence and
   overlap checks, create one task-only ticket and update only that ticket plus
   this handover, then stop for ticket-specific `ask_user` approval with comments.
10. Before each implementation commit, inspect the exact diff, run focused and
    boundary checks, verify staged allowlist/secret hygiene, and label it cleanup
    or fix. For the final fix commit, move the ticket to done and record final
    outcome/validation. Handover-only control commits contain only this file and
    record post-commit user/transition decisions.

## Severity transition gates

| Transition | Findings entered | Status | Required action | Decision record |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Program start → critical | F-001–F-004 | active; no gate | Begin directly in critical | D-011 |
| critical → high | F-005–F-011 | approved | Enter high and prepare F-005 only | D-035 |
| high → medium | F-012–F-037 | approved | Enter medium and prepare F-012 only | D-084 |
| medium → low | F-038–F-058 | blocked by medium; pending | Complete/close medium work, then `ask_user` with comment entry enabled | none |

A transition gate authorizes entry into the next severity band, not a ticket's
implementation. Every ticket ordinarily requires its own `ask_user` approval with comment
entry enabled after creation. D-111 temporarily waives that prompt for F-015
through F-024 only. Comments and conditions from either decision are binding
and must be copied into the decision log and relevant ticket entry.
When a completed ticket is the last work in a band, its completion-review gate
must be accepted first; only then may the distinct band-transition gate be asked.

## Ticket registry

Seventeen task-only tickets have been materialized. F-001 through F-017 are
completion-accepted. Temporary one-ticket-at-a-time batch execution continues
through F-024 without per-ticket approval or completion prompts, subject to
D-111 stop conditions; F-018 is next.

Use these statuses: `not created`, `awaiting ticket approval`, `ticket revision
requested`, `approved`, `cleanup discovery`, `cleanup review`, `cleanup fixing`,
`fix implementation`, `fix review`, `fixing`, `blocked`, `completed`, `committed`,
`awaiting completion review`, `completion changes requested`, `accepted;
continuation authorized`.

Each real entry must include: ticket path/title; findings; severity; status;
ticket approval decision/date/comments; cleanup-discovery result; durable
in-progress commit; ordered commit sequence with sequence number, `cleanup` or
`fix` type, hash/subject, scope, review/fix result, validation, controller check,
and affected F-ids; final outcome/validation; final done commit; completion-
review presentation; user acceptance/date/comments and authorization to
continue; related control commits; next step.

### 2026-07-19T13:24:58+02:00 — F-003 final fix completed

  the admitted mutex, canonical path, and backend identity remain authoritative.
  Encrypted backend creation and open-existing behavior are explicit, and
  rotation performs no `StoreRecord` save.

  rollback restores the old canonical generation while retaining unpublished
  new `stage/`; unrecoverable rollback preserves useful paths, conditionally
  locks the admitted entry, and returns HTTP 500 code
  `rotation_recovery_required` without fallback or stale publication.

  complete generations indefinitely until deliberate trusted-host cleanup.
  Browser acknowledgement is local dismissal only and is not evidence that a
  key was received, saved, verified, or placed in external custody.

  with a host before credential checks, backend construction/network activity,
  or wrapper-socket binding. `BackendClient::open` repeats that admission before
  credential-file reads or worker creation, and the production reqwest builder
  is HTTPS-only. Existing endpoint concatenation preserves non-root base paths
  and trailing slashes. The NixOS module independently requires lowercase
  `https://` with an instance-specific assertion.

  ordering, direct-client bypass resistance, and base-path preservation. A
  repository-PKI HTTPS endpoint redirects the production client to a plaintext
  trap and proves zero connections. The committed
  `frontend-backend-url-eval` check uses equivalent self-contained HTTP/HTTPS
  NixOS fixtures, proves only HTTP toplevel evaluation fails, and records the
  exact generated HTTPS base-path URL. The deployed VM gives a standalone daemon
  unusable credential paths plus a plaintext URL and proves a numeric nonzero,
  non-timeout exit, exact diagnostic, absent socket/readiness, and zero trap
  accepts while the normal generated HTTPS/mTLS flows still exercise
  registration, resource reads, policy, and writeback.

  downgrade regression could connect to the plaintext listener and then hang
  while the test waited for `register` to return. A separate fix worker made the

  connection assertion. Two fresh pass-2 concurrency and final-contract

  rustfmt, and diff checks pass. One immediate back-to-back workspace invocation

  `last_used`; its isolated rerun and the complete workspace rerun passed. Nix

  descendant memory/environment, private files/tmpfs, D-Bus/libsecret state, and
  invocation descriptors. The untrusted caller or service manager can terminate
  same-UID `underwrap-exec`; either trusted supervisor can also crash or be
  killed. Direction is caller/service-manager → `underwrap-exec` → host
  `underwrap-ns` → namespace PID-1 relay → complete secret-bearing namespace.
  Actual outer-supervisor death must eventually leave no live namespace process,
  subject to kernel scheduling/uninterruptible-sleep and root/kernel exclusions.

  orphan but initially differed over retaining the configured target as PID 1.
  Authoritative Linux `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG(2)` and `pid_namespaces(7)` semantics and
  a fresh oracle established that fork/credential/secure-file-capability exec
  can clear PDEATHSIG and namespace PID 1 sees an outside parent as PPID 0.
  Select a trusted non-execing, dedicated-target-UID, zero-capability
  `underwrap-ns` relay as PID 1 and the configured target as PID 2. Link
  `underwrap-exec` to host `underwrap-ns` with one fixed private liveness fd and
  link host supervisor to relay with a second private liveness pipe; race-close
  both with `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG(SIGKILL)`. Return the direct PID-2 target/preexec
  status through one exact private bounded channel.

  requires F-006 gate acceptance, one exact direct-child status, and clean relay
  reap. A valid pre-exec child status still propagates when only the gate
  suppresses resource finalization. Synchronized cancellation or relay failure
  established before dispatch sends neither `Checkin` nor `UsageSignal`. A
  terminal request completed before later stage-1 death cannot be retracted;
  without an abort/transaction there is an explicit dispatch-to-wrapper-exit
  race. Checkout/open and `last_used_at` are not rolled back.

## Cross-ticket constraints and invariants

- Preserve the threat model: the caller is untrusted with respect to brokered
  secrets; the configured target is trusted with its projection but its Secret
  Service input is untrusted for robustness/storage integrity; root, kernel,
  root-owned configuration, and backend are trusted.
- Dedicated per-`(instance, wrapper)` target UIDs remain the primary `/proc`
  confidentiality boundary. Never fall back to caller or root identity.
- Runtime clients stay at wrapper/resource vocabulary and cannot name storage
  keys or `control/*`; the backend remains policy authority.
- Secret material never enters argv, inter-stage environment, shell commands,
  logs, tickets, or this handover. Persistent brokered resources use only the
  backend `StorageBackend`, except documented namespace-private tmpfs staging.
- Security mechanisms fail closed. Do not weaken/remove an assertion to make a
  test pass. Ask before adding a dependency, changing the
  `underwrap-exec → underwrap-ns` chain, or weakening a security boundary.
- Cleanup adds stricter guardrails: it is always behavior-preserving and may
  never weaken a security boundary, even with user approval. Behavior-changing
  work belongs only in separately approved fix/Opportunity scope. Dependency,
  binary-chain, secret-exposure, and unresolved-decision gates remain binding.
  Each cleanup must clearly reduce complexity, duplication, or dead structure,
  be independently reversible, and avoid valueless formatting/renaming.
- Use fix-led severity sequencing. Do not perform a one-time repository-wide
  refactor; seek repository-wide simplification only in the cleanup phase of an
  approved current ticket and commit accepted units before its fix.
- Immediately record every cleanup effect on another F-id and its commit. Later
  revalidate/link that work instead of duplicating a ticket.
- After every completed implementation sequence, block next-ticket preparation
  until the user accepts completion and authorizes continuation through
  `ask_user` with comments. Persist that response—and any later transition
  response—in a handover-only control commit. A requested completion change
  reopens the same ticket; a following severity transition remains separate.
  D-111 temporarily supersedes only the per-ticket approval/completion prompts
  for F-015 through F-024; all stop conditions and the severity-transition gate
  remain mandatory, and ordinary prompts resume at F-025.
- Re-evaluate permission mode on open/finalize and writable on submitted
  writeback. Preserve documented last-writer-wins unless separately approved;
  never claim CAS without it.
- Files and libsecret projections remain writable locally even in read-only
  policy; read-only means changes do not persist.
- Follow current-state, greenfield documentation rules and required vocabulary.
  Do not add migration history or ticket planning to published docs.
- Use report themes/dependencies for coherent work, not mechanically one ticket
  per finding and not miscellaneous grab bags.
- Revalidate report evidence after intervening changes and link overlap rather
  than duplicate active or cleanup-completed work.
- NixOS module or privileged wrapper-chain boundary changes require the VM test;
  apply focused/integration/browser checks required by `AGENTS.md` and the skill.

## Accumulated lessons

- Test helpers should return only the capability current tests consume; retaining
  decoded response metadata behind dead-code suppressions obscures test intent.
- Removing an unused test-only scope helper narrows F-043 evidence but does not
  address its production no-op wire/environment field or duplicate config. Later
  F-043 work must revalidate production evidence and link cleanup sequence 2.
- Cleanup discovery rejected abstractions that would consolidate the current
  lossy F-001 path; simplification must not make an imminent defect easier to
  preserve.
- A lossy flat projection is suitable only as a read/list view. Slot-scoped
  mutation must select and update the canonical typed aggregate so unrelated
  sections survive the load-modify-save cycle.
- Test fixtures should model one instance socket and one unified listener as
  singleton capabilities rather than duplicate them per wrapper or request role.
- Rare parallel integration authentication failures may be consistent with the
  pre-existing release-before-bind port race, but this is not proven and does not
  authorize O-024 fixture redesign; preserve the evidence for later review.
- A bulk outer frame bound does not constrain an inner vector soon enough: read
  and validate the inner resource length before allocating/copying its body, and
  retain message-level checks even when outer arithmetic would admit the payload.
- Validate the fully composed target environment, including NUL-free values and
  valid keys from resource, fixed, and passthrough sources, during pure host
  planning before namespace, mount, staging, helper, UID, or target effects.
- Exact-one journal evidence must observe arrival, allow a bounded post-completion
  ingestion interval, then re-query the entire cursor-scoped set; stopping at the
  first record proves existence, not uniqueness.
- Structured audit sinks must encode operator-facing metadata for the sink:
  journald values containing newlines require native binary framing, while stderr
  fallback requires valid one-line JSON escaping. Trusted operator input is not a
  reason to corrupt audit structure.
- Complete-resource limits must be budgeted independently from publication-body,
  generic-control, HTTP/base64, message-envelope, and outer-prefix bounds. ACK
  size eligibility remains distinct from caller receipt and backend durability.
- A NixOS hardening assertion must inspect the final typed configuration consumed
  by a generator, not only a convenience option that contributes to it. When a
  freeform consumer grammar is security-relevant, use a simple fail-closed
  superset that cannot miss accepted spellings, while retaining the consumer's
  swap-versus-filesystem distinction to avoid unrelated boundary expansion.
- A default public trait convenience method with no call, override, macro use, or
  workspace consumer still widens the API and error surface. Under the greenfield
  posture, exhaustive reachability plus full workspace validation can justify
  deleting that dead structure as an independently reversible cleanup.
- Entity lifecycle checks must enumerate the canonical typed aggregate rather
  than a deliberately lossy convenience projection. Ordered multi-key deletion
  can prevent false success without pretending to be a transaction: call later
  operations only after earlier ones report success, and re-read state after any
  storage error because an error is not proof of invisibility.
- A modified detached worktree must not share a Cargo target with authoritative
  main-tree validation without an explicit affected-package clean: Cargo can
  reuse a red-probe test binary despite clean main-tree source. Prefer an
  isolated target directory, then remove the worktree and verify source/status.
- Greenfield store accessors consumed only by tests written for those accessors
  are dead API, but delete them only while preserving independently useful model
  evidence directly. Do not delete fixture tests merely because end-to-end flows
  overlap partially; unique construction assertions remain valuable.
- A zeroizing candidate guard covers values it still owns on failure, but a
  mutation that deletes or overwrites a candidate vector must wipe that vector
  before removal. Keep secretsd live-memory commit after exact ACK distinct from
  host ACK-write eligibility: response loss can preserve host finalization
  eligibility without authorizing a secretsd commit or proving backend durability.
- Referential-integrity integration fixtures must create real published clients
  and complete store-local resource/pair graphs; dangling fixture rows can hide
  the production gate being tested. Seed content without policy when a later
  remap is itself the behavior under test, then grant only after the mapping is
  valid.
- A typed serde error response can ignore unknown JSON fields. When evidence must
  prove a value-free exact public envelope, compare the complete parsed JSON
  value (or exact bytes), not only the expected typed fields.

After each later unit, add concise reusable facts without duplicating raw review
transcripts or secret-bearing data.

  server session is revoked; failure must remain visible and retryable.

  Unicode writes and reads neither throw unexpectedly nor corrupt exact secret
  bytes.

  then test that host processes cannot see or connect to the private socket.
- **F-030 — Security-critical Nix assertions lack a negative evaluation matrix.** Priority:
  `medium`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Add expected-failure module
  evaluations for every security-critical assertion family so removal or
  inversion of fail-closed checks cannot remain hidden behind valid-only tests.
- **F-031 — Several privileged module input shapes fail only at service start or invocation.** Priority:
  `medium`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Privileged names, paths, CA
  inputs, access paths, and generated-table values need checked Nix shapes plus
  runtime defense so operator mistakes fail at evaluation with specific errors.
- **F-032 — Security-critical unsafe blocks usually lack explicit invariant rationale.** Priority:
  `medium`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Perform semantic review of
  each production unsafe site and state its pointer, alignment, lifetime,
  descriptor-ownership, privilege, or syscall invariant; comments supplement
  rather than replace tests.
- **F-033 — Privileged namespace crate inherits broad backend/UI/tooling dependencies.** Priority:
  `medium`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Narrow the privileged crate's
  shared dependency surface so it consumes only the canonical domain and local
  ABI portions it needs, without claiming dead-code dependencies already expand
  the runtime TCB.
- **F-034 — Offline administrator lockout recovery is undocumented.** Priority:
  `medium`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Document the shipped,
  quiesced, stdin-based offline recovery flow, including service identity,
  non-overwrite behavior, and unchanged bootstrap sentinel, without placing a
  password in argv.
- **F-035 — Wrapper resource name is mislabeled as an internal stable id.**
  Priority: `medium`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Use “operator-facing
  resource name” consistently for wrapper configuration and reserve “resource
  id” for stable backend records so operators do not bind the wrong concept.
- **F-036 — Dialog and drawer close controls hide keyboard focus.** Priority:
  `medium`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Give dialog and drawer close
  controls the shared visible keyboard-focus treatment and verify it across
  themes and forced-colors behavior.
- **F-037 — Per-store pages retain stale ids and misdiagnose list failures.**
  Priority: `medium`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Reconcile stale URL
  store IDs after lists resolve and show list failures before accurate empty
  states, rather than stranding operators or presenting an inapplicable grant
  diagnosis.

### Low

- **F-038 — JCS safe-integer limit is claimed but not enforced.** Priority:
  `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Enforce the documented JCS-safe
  timestamp integer range in both serialization directions and reject values
  above it, preserving cross-implementation canonical behavior. F-008 final fix
  `a1ee6cf1392a807fc88bc7a56347270c402e044c` bounds timestamps only for
  host-selected published libsecret generations; generic `ResourceBlob` ingress/
  egress remains, so F-038 is
  narrowed but unresolved and must later link/revalidate that work.
- **F-039 — Item setters leave Collection.Modified stale.** Priority: `low`;
  Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Every item mutation must update both
  the item and containing collection modification timestamps consistently.
- **F-040 — secretsd-store Rustdoc describes a superseded socket-only flow.**
  Priority: `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. F-008 final fix
  `a1ee6cf1392a807fc88bc7a56347270c402e044c`
  removes namespace-private state-file persistence and publishes canonical
  in-memory state over the invocation-private secretsd-to-host stream, while the
  unchanged crate Rustdoc still says “daemon channel (authenticated socketpair
  fd).” F-040 remains unresolved; later work must link F-008 and describe the
  current in-memory/private-publication flow rather than either superseded design.
- **F-041 — “Only persistent secret store” overstates brokered-resource scope.** Priority:
  `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Scope the persistence claim to
  brokered resource material and accurately enumerate separately controlled TLS
  and runtime identity credentials, without implying their current permissions
  are weak.
- **F-042 — Daemon is documented as opaque to a blob it parses.** Priority:
  `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. State accurately that the daemon
  parses the complete resource blob and extracts env while files/libsecret
  semantics remain opaque, keeping that parser in security review scope.
- **F-043 — No-op scope and duplicated helper fields obscure authority.**
  Priority: `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Cleanup sequence 2
  (`35f93926ba3ef0147d140b6b1d163c5729606ed7`) removes one unused test-only
  scope helper and narrows this evidence; the production caller scope and
  duplicate namespace-helper configuration remain. Later F-043 work must
  revalidate and link that cleanup rather than repeat it.
- **F-044 — Resource env can override forced XDG paths.** Priority: `low`;
  Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Reserve all forced XDG variables and
  preserve forced-value precedence so trusted resource data cannot contradict
  documented target runtime/config paths.
- **F-045 — SetAlias persists malformed or dangling references.** Priority:
  `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Alias updates must validate a
  bounded path segment, exact collection path, and existing target before
  changing canonical Secret Service object state. F-008 final fix
  `a1ee6cf1392a807fc88bc7a56347270c402e044c` keeps
  malformed/dangling aliases out of the host-selected backend generation, but
  pre-mutation handler validation, exact `InvalidArgs`, poisoned in-memory state,
  and alias registration remain; F-045 is narrowed but unresolved and must later
  link/revalidate that work.
- **F-046 — CreateItem wrong-typed properties silently become empty values.**
  Priority: `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Distinguish absent
  item properties from wrong-typed values and reject invalid variants rather
  than silently converting them to empty metadata that changes replacement
  behavior.
- **F-047 — Profile text states the opposite session-revocation behavior.**
  Priority: `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Make profile guidance
  say that a password change invalidates other sessions on their next request
  and verify the behavior with separate browser contexts.
- **F-048 — Confirmation failures emit unhandled promise rejections.** Priority:
  `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. The shared confirmation flow
  must consume expected mutation rejection after displaying its inline error so
  normal API failures do not become unhandled browser promise rejections.
- **F-049 — Internal backend error chains are returned and rendered.** Priority:
  `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Keep detailed correlated errors
  in server-side logs and return stable public 5xx codes/messages so
  authenticated admins do not receive host paths or implementation chains.
- **F-050 — Playwright fixture retains removed master-key configuration.**
  Priority: `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Remove stale ignored
  key-directory configuration and comments from the browser fixture so it
  reflects one-time external key custody and does not teach a false model.
- **F-051 — Published docs contain ticket workflow/dead path and temporal wording.** Priority:
  `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Remove contributor workflow and
  dead-path material from published product docs and describe only the intended
  present first-release design.
- **F-052 — Audit/writeback comments retain unsupported format and vocabulary.** Priority:
  `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Update Rustdoc and comments to
  describe actual tracing fields and sinks and use current permission/resource
  vocabulary, so incident responders do not search for nonexistent formats or
  events.
- **F-053 — Tablet Playwright project discovers no tests.** Priority: `low`;
  Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Add focused tablet tests matching the
  configured project and intended viewport so the project represents real
  evidence rather than an empty label.
- **F-054 — Log-hygiene test misses encoded/full-blob disclosure.** Priority:
  `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Strengthen log checks with
  per-run canaries across relevant journals and common encodings/canonical blob
  forms, without printing the canaries or secret-bearing content.
- **F-055 — Remote VM scenario is loopback-only with stale SSH setup.** Priority:
  `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Remove unused SSH setup and
  describe the existing test honestly as one-node loopback transport evidence;
  do not claim DNS, firewall, or cross-host coverage it does not provide.
- **F-056 — Strict rustdoc currently fails and no doc gate exists.** Priority:
  `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Fix current denied-warning
  Rustdoc failures and add a strict workspace documentation gate so public API
  documentation drift cannot remain green.
- **F-057 — Rust 2024 workspace opts out of resolver 3 without an MSRV contract.** Priority:
  `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Declare the supported Rust
  version and document the intentional resolver choice, then verify dependency
  resolution and tests without treating this maintenance ambiguity as a runtime
  defect.
- **F-058 — Frontend/OpenAPI versions trail current supported lines.** Priority:
  `low`; Status: `not ticketed`; Ticket: `none`. Refresh tested browser and
  frontend tooling within compatible supported lines and record the support
  matrix; version age and valid OpenAPI 3.1.0 alone are not defects.

## Compact update template

Append or update the registry, ledger, decision log, ordered commit sequence, and
lessons at each stage; do not merely paste this template without resolving it.

```markdown
### <date/time> — <ticket path/title or program event>
- Stage: transition gate | ticket created | ticket revision | ticket approval |
  cleanup discovery | cleanup unit N | cleanup review/fix N | fix unit N |
  fix review/fix N | completed | committed | completion review |
  completion changes requested | continuation authorized | control commit | blocked
- Findings/status: <F-ids and ledger transition; other F-ids whose evidence was
  changed/resolved>
- Severity/transition: <current band; transition decision ID/comments if any>
- Ticket approval: <explicit ask_user decision/date/comments or pending>
- Scope/dependencies: <coherent boundary; prerequisite exception/decision ID>
- Cleanup discovery: <repository-wide and ticket-local areas examined; accepted
  simplifications or why none had concrete value; guardrail check>
- Unit/type: <sequence N; cleanup or fix; independently reversible scope>
- Outcome/diff: <what changed; exact relevant paths; intentional behavior remains
  unchanged for cleanup>
- Cross-finding effect: <affected F-id, evidence effect, and commit/link; or none;
  never imply Opportunity/U-item approval>
- Review/fix: <fresh reviewer evidence; controller synthesis; separate fix-worker
  result; accepted/rejected corrections and reasons>
- Controller check: <acceptance, sanity, scope, actual diff, security invariants,
  independent reversibility for cleanup, and unrelated-change check>
- Validation: <focused and boundary commands/results; unavailable checks and
  next-best evidence>
- Board/handover state: <todo/in-progress/done; first post-approval commit makes
  in-progress durable; final fix commit makes done/final outcome durable>
- Commit: <ordered implementation sequence; `cleanup` or `fix`; `SELF` plus
  subject for the commit containing this handover revision, later resolved by a
  control commit; exact allowlist and secret inspection confirmed>
- Control commit: <handover-only hash/subject recording a completion/transition
  decision and resolving preceding SELF placeholders; or none/pending>
- Final outcome/validation: <required on the final fix commit; otherwise pending>
- Completion review: <presentation of outcome, ordered commits, validation,
  reviewer result, controller sanity check, handover decisions, later-finding
  effects; ask_user decision/date/comments; explicit acceptance and continuation
  authorization, `completion changes requested`, or pending. Requested changes
  reopen this same ticket and prohibit next-ticket preparation>
- Gate ordering: <no next-ticket preparation before completion acceptance; if a
  band transition follows, its separate gate occurs afterward>
- Lessons/constraints: <facts that must inform later tickets>
- Next action/blocker: <single next protocol step; ask_user decision if required>
```
